WARGRAN E" NATIONAL SCHOOL:

, The Rev. F. Grenstead, Diocesan Inspector, who conducted a recent examination in religious know- ledge, at the above school, has forwarded his report to the Rev. J. Caison,rector,which reads as follows: —The religious tone of this school is strongly marked ; the prayers are very excellent, and the hymns are very nicely sung. The inspection has also shown that the instruction imparted is earnest and thorough. Group 1. Passed a creditable ex- amination their Bible being well-known, the old' Testament, on the whole, better than the hew; the catechism is also accurately repeated, and their explanation of it with reference to the scripture freely given. The repetition of Scripture both here and in all the groups is unequal, some passages being thoroughly mastered,others more imperfectly grasped. Group '2. did excellent work, their accur- ate verbal memory of passages from St. John's Gospel showing how well it had been taught. Cate- chism and prayer book were also very good; old Testament rather weaker. The written work of , both this group and group 3 deserves special com- mendation. Group 3. The answering here was un- equal. There is some excellent work done, but the answering should be more general amongst the lower half of the class. With this exception the work is good. As a whole the examination shows a .very satisfactory condition of religious education in the school. Present 232, average 225. FUNERAL OF MR. JOHN RODGERS. _ _ _ . _

The interment:of the body of Mr. John Rodgers, Wargrave, whose death we recorded last week, took place on Saturday. Owing to the body having to be conveyed to Glasgow, the funeral cortege left deceased's late residence at the early hour of 8 o'clock, for Earlestown station. Amongst those who followed the hearse to the station were Mr. James and George Rodgers, Mr. William Sinclair, nephew, Mr. W. Crawford, Rev. I. &Hatt, and other friends and neighbours. Representing the Vulcan Foundry Co. where deceased was employed. were Mr. Collingwood (general manager), Mr. Norman, works manager, Mr. Bodmer, Mr. Thompson, the foreman, and the employes of the smithy. The coffin was of oak, with brass mountings, and was covered with fine black cloth, in accordance with the Scotch custom. The inscription on the plate was—" John Rodgers, died starch 21, 1895, aged 47 years." Beautiful wreaths were sent by "The employees of th.e Vulcan Foundry Co.;' Mr. and Mrs. Crawford, Mr. and Mrs. Porter. On arrival at the station the coffin was placed in a vehicle, provided by the Railway Company, which was afterwards attached to the 8.45 train for the North. On arrival at Glasgow at 4.10 in the afternoon, the corpse was met by the relatives of the deceased and widow, and amidst a heavy downpour of rain the cortege drove to Sighthill Cemetery, where it was met by about 150 of the deceased's late fellow workmen, friends and acquainta.nces, who accompanied the body to its last resting place. The service at the grave was conducted by the Rev. Mr. Johnston of the United Presbyterian Church, Springburn, Glasgow.

SONS OP TEMPERANCE.

The Samuel Morley Section of Cadets held their usual meeting on Wednesday evening in the Co- operative Hall. Earlestown, Bro. W. Gibbs, W.A., presided, and Bro. o.Pickett. A.W.P. took charge of the section in the absence of Bro. Judson through sickness. Shortly before closing time the worthy patron visited the section. On his entrance in the room the section gave him a hearty reception. It was reported that a section was to be opened at Tiaydock on Friday the 29th, to be named the Sir Wilfred Lawson section.—The Britain Need Division met at 7.30, Bro S. Foden, W.P., in the chair. There was a very good atter.dance, it being quarterly night. The following were elected as representatives to Grand Division which will meet at Earlestown on April 20th :—Bros. John Judson, W P., J. French, P.W P., J. Pickett, P.W P., S. B'oden, W P., b. H. Robinson. R.G. ; alternate rerre- .4entatives, T. Judson, P.W.P., J. Hughes, P.W.P. After discussion it was decided to have a picnic,and the following were elected a committee to meet representatives of the tern perance societies to unite: Bro. Pearson, G. Clarke, and S. Foden. A very pleasant and profitable meeting closed about 9.30.

THE FUNERAL.

The funeral of Mr. Joseph Davies took place at the Cemetery on Tuesday. A large number of friends and representatives from the Corporation and public institu- tions assembled to pay their last tribut6 of respect to their fellow-townsman. The funeral cortege left the Old Warps at eleven o'clock, and at Bridge Foot it was met by members of the Corporation and borough magistrates who had proceeded from the Town Hall in carriages,and were headed by the police, under the direction of Mr. S. Hunt (chief constable), and Inspectors Cox and Bowen, and the children from the Blue Coat School,in charge of Mr. E. Rose. The funeral. procession then proceeded along Bridge-st.,Buttermarket-st.,lrlam-st.,and Church- St. to the Parish Church. There were 35 carriages in procession and the officiating clergymen at the Parish Church were the Rev. F. Slater (vicar of St. James's), the Rev.G. B. Thurston, and the Rev. H.M.R. Hutt. The procession was met at the Parish Church by the clergy and choir, with the people's warden, Council- lor A. Woods,and upon entering the building, the "Dead March" in Saul was played by Mr.W.S. Woods.theorgan- ' ist. The hymn, " Brief life is here our portion," was then'sung, followed by the 90th Psalm. After the lesson had been read by the Rev. F. Slater, the hymn, " Abide , with me," was sung, after which the choir led the way out of the church, singing, " Lord, now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace." The procession then proceeded to the Ceme- tery, and the concluding cerennony was performed by the Rev. G. B. Thurston, after which the children of the Blue Coat School sang in a very impressive manner, "0 God our help in ages past." The deceased gentleman I was buried in a vault on the north side of the Cemetery a granite monument standing at the head, in memory of" Edwin Joseph Davies, son of Joseph and Betsy Davies, born 14th March, 1854, died August 25th 1882 ; I also Dennis Joseph Davies, another son,died 12th August 1868, aged 7 years. The chief mourners were Mr. John E. Davies, Mr. IPercy Davies, and Mr. Harry Jones Davies, (sons), Masters Denis, and Leicester Davies (grandsons) Mr. W. Fairhurst (brother-in-law), Mr. B. Hall (Southport), Mr. Joseph Acton (Sutton Weaver),Mr. Forshaw (Messrs. Davies, and Forshaw), and Mr. John Burgess. The members and officials of the Corporation present were the Mayor ( L ouncillor James Fairclough), Alder- men Francomb, Harrison, Blackhurst, Wright and Hutchinson, Councillors Roberts Baird, Longton,Woods, Smethurst, Bennett, Cannel', Pierpoint, Monks, Evans, R. T. Fairclough, Heaton, Hill, Lea, Muir, Plumpton, Shillcock, Skelton, and Smethurst, Messrs. J. Lyon Whittle (town clerk), J. Fairhurst (borough treasarer), T. Longdin (borough surveyor), F. Taylor (secretary of gas department), Ross (water works department). The magistracy were represented by Messrs. John Fairclough, R. Garnett, T. Birchall, J. H. Fleming, J. W. Hayward, S. Chandly, J. Geddes, G.F.F. White (deputy magistrates' clerk). The Liberal Association were represented by Messrs. Pierpoint, Roberts, and Fleming, and the Chamber of Commerce by Messrs. Fleming, Garnett, Pierpoint, John Fairclough, Roberts, and Bennett. The Lancashire and Yorkshire Bank was represented by Mr. H. S. Crompton (general manager). and Mr. F. W. Gilbert (local manager), Carr's Colliery Company, Prescott, were re- presented by Mr. H. R. D. A. Willis, J.P.. DL., of Halsnead, Prescot, and Mr. Edward Fidler (directors), and Messrs. A. Read, J. Atherton, J. Read, P. Jones, T. Barrow,and J. Saunders. Mr. H. D. Spencer represented the Union Plate Glass works, St. Helens. _ _

Starkey Smith, White, Sherratt, Fox, Richmond,and Spinks, Messrs. Sylvanus Reynolds. Longland, E. Connor, C. Worthington, J. L. Tunstall, C. D. Parkinson, R. Dobson, W. D. Jeans, W. Tinsley, T. Mawdsley (representing Newton Conserva- tive Association), Highfield, H. N. Houghton,S. Davies, J. Knowles, A. Jolley, W. Dillmore, W. Lowe, If. B. Matthews, H. W. Cookson, J. T. Picton, J. R. Cookson. Wreaths were sent by Mrs. Joseph Davies, Mr. and Mrs. J. E. Davies, Mr. and Mrs. Percy Davies and children, home and farm servants, nurse, Carr's Colliery Company, the workmen of Carr's Colliery Company, Mrs. Sylvanus Reynolds, Mr. and Mrs. %aye, Mr. and Mrs. John Geddes, Mr. and Mrs. Forshaw, Messrs. Mounfield and Sons, and was of dark pollard oak, with pannelled sides and brass mountings, the inscription being : " Joseph Davies, of the Old Warps, born November 24th, 1826, died March 22nd, 1895." The funeral ar- rangements ware admirably carried out by Messrs. J. and W. Dutton, Sankey-street.

MAGISTERIAL REFERENCE.

On Saturday morning at the Warrington Borough Pohie Court, Mr. John Burgess, the Chairman, said be- fore proceeding with the business of the court, he would make observation of an event that had taken place within the last few hours, and that was the death of an esteemed and honoured colleague of theirs on the Bench. He referred to Mr. Joseph Davies, of the Old Warps, Latchford, a gentleman who had taken an exceedingly active part in promoting to the best of his ability and with the best judgment that be could bring to bear, the various insfitutioas in this borough and which were flourishing, very largely threugh his instrumentality. The speaker mentioned the Infirmary, which he said owed its existence very largely indeed to the action taken by Mr. Davies. The acquisition af the Town Hall Was another of Mr. Davies's works, for he was the prin- cipal agent in arranging with Lord Winmarleigh for the transfer of the property to the borough, and Mr. Burgess thought Warrington to-day had great cause to be grati- fied that Mr. Davies took the part he did. He (Mr. Bur- gess) hardly knew of anything that was worth taking charge of that Mr. Davies had not been associated with ia his day, and they had lost in Warrington a man that c mid not easily be replaced. THE CHAMBER OF COMMERCE AND MR. DAVIES. It was unanimously resolved at the meeting of the Council of the Chamber of Commerce, on Monday, that the members record their sincere regret at the death of Mr. Joseph Davies, J.P., who was one of the founders of the Chamber and who for many years took an active interest in its affairs. The Council further desired to express its sympathy with Mrs. Davies and the bereaved family, and appointed a deputation, consisting of the President (Mr. J. H. Fleming) the Vice-President (Mr. Robert Garnett), and Messrs. John Fairclough, William Pierpoint, Henry Roberts, and Arthur Bennett, to attend the funeral of the deceased gentleman.

BURTONWOOD SCHOOL BOARD.

The quarterly meeting of the above Board was held in the classroom of the Board School, Burton- wood, at 7.15 p.m. on Tuesday last, the 26th inst. Present: Messrs. Andrew Jackson (chairman), William Fairclough, Thomas Norcott, Rev. A. M. Mitchell, 11. Gawthorpe (schoolmaster), F. Wright (attendance officer), William Webster (clerk). The Clerk produced the estimate of receipts and expenditure for the next six month, and it was resolved to serve a pre- cept en the overseers for £72 19s. being at the rate of Id. in the S. The schoolmaster reported the breakage of numerous panes of glass in the school windows, and that it was evident from the marks on the walls and other indications that the damage was wilful. The Clerk was instructed to communi- cate with the police with a view to the apprehen- sion and prosecution of the offenders. The attend- ance officer reported he had made on an average 10 visits per week since the previous rneeting,and that there was still room for improvement in the matter of attendance.

At Adelaide, on Thursday, Mr. Stoddart's cricket team commenced the final match of their tour, their oppon- ents being an eleven of South Australia. The kustralians batted first, acd when stumps were diawn for the day the score stood at 233 for seven wickets. It was stated at a meeting of the shareholders on Thursday that the loss to the North British Railway Company, directly and indirectly, through the Scotch coal strike was £170,000. The Recordership of Newcastle-on-Tyne, made vacan by the death of Mr. Digby Seymour, Q.C., has been conferred on Mr. W. S. Robson, Q.C.

A Darmstadt telegram says Queen Victoria is expected to arrive there from Nice about the 20th April.

Ma. GLADSTONE.-Mr. Gladstone has, since his return ts England.been again inundated with correspondence. Ile, therefore, desires to intimate through the Press that he cannot undertake either to read or to answer any let- ters which may be addressed to him.

FATAL FILM IN COUNTY DowN.—Otter Lodge, Dro- more, county Down, was destroyed by fire on Thursday morning. The inmates had a narrow escape. Two tres- passers, a man and a woman, were found dead in an outhouse badly burned, and it is thought that they had set the place on fire. Twenty-two cows and three horses were roasted to death.

ALLEGED EXTENSIVE EMBEZZLEMENTS BY A LIVERPOOL CLERK.—John Matthew Keene, 24, clerk in the employ- ment of Mews. Elder, Dempster and Co., steamship owners, was, at Liverpool, on Thursday, committed to the sessions charged with embezzling £1,700. The pri- soner had absconded to America,where he was arrested. Over £l,OOO in gold was found in his possession.

NOTICE IS HEREBY GIVEN, that the next GENERAL 'QUARTER SESSIONS of the Peace for the COUNTY OFCHESTER will be held at the COURT HOUSh, NETHER KNUTSFORD, in and for the said County, on MONDAY, the Bth day of April next, at half- past eleven o'clock in the Forenoon, when the Grand Jury will be empannelled. The hearing of all Appeals arising within the Hundreds of Bucklow. Macclesfield, and Northwith, forming the Koutaford District, and the trials af all prisoners com- mitted from such Hundreds, will take place at such Sessions. The Grand and Petty Juries for the Knuts- ford District must be in attendance at the Court House, Knutsford, atthe opening of the Court on MONDAY, the Bth day of April next, at half-past eleven o'clock in the Forenoon. THE Court will be Adjourned to the Castle of Chester, on THURSDAY, the 11th day of April next, at eleven o'clock in the Forenoon. The hearing of all Appeals' arising within the Hundreds of Braxton, Eddisbury, Nantwich„ and Wirral, forming the Chester District,and the trials of all prisoners committed from the last-men- tioned Hundreds will take place at such Adjourned Sessions. The Grand and Petty Juries for the Chester District must be in attendance at the Castle of Chester, at the opening of the Court on THURSDAY, the 11th day of April next, at eleven o'clock in the Forenoon. REGINALD POTTB, Clerk of the Peace for the County of Cheater. Chester, 18th March, 1895. •
